# Source extracts and audit trail

The 389 source-note sections are reproduced below. Cited transcription pages are then reproduced once per package/scan, in full. This permits a subsequent line-by-line audit; inclusion is not itself a check event.

## SA02-001 — MPL_84_097-145#1

**Heading:** Source and context conventions

**Index kind:** 

**Scans cited:** 

### Source and context conventions

These notes were written from the frozen transcription, after the context was opened; no image was inspected and no transcription was changed for this research. Source paths below are relative to `MPL_84_097-145/`; the scan number is the archive scan, and line numbers are approximate lines of that individual frozen TeX file. The supplied `context/same_cote_84/MPL_84_146-193_notes.md` is abbreviated **C84** in the metadata, always with its item and scan reference; it and its entries in `context/notes/RESULTS_STATE.md` are earlier research claims/leads, not automatically reviewed results.

**RS-product** means **Hua, findings register, `84-product-quadratic-algebras-formula`**; **RS-χ** means **Hua, findings register, `84-chi-trivialised-extension-product`**; **RS-later** means the supplied register ID **`MPL_84_146-193 §2`**. “No direct RS entry” means that the supplied register does not state the particular claim, not that the wider literature was searched. Each A-item has a corresponding, actually executed context search in `research/algebra_context_searches.md`; proofs and reproducible calculations are in `research/algebra_derivations.md`, `research/algebra_checks.json`, and `research/relative_norm_checks.json`.

The allowed kind describes the source passage or the new research contribution, not an automatic verdict on its truth. Author-marked pending steps, including those later discharged, are separately registered in `research/algebra_unverified_steps.md`. No LM2/GT/congruence implication is inferred from the occurrence of norms, involutions, or \(\mathbf F_2\).

## SA02-002 — MPL_84_097-145#2

**Heading:** A01 — The extension duality includes a checked minus sign

**Index kind:** self-flagged. **Brief exact quotation:** `"g","?"`; `Attention au \unsure{signe}`; `dès que $\unsure{\varphi}$ est iso`.

**Scans cited:** 97,160,174

### A01 — The extension duality includes a checked minus sign

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan097.tex`, scan 097, approximately lines 3–45. **Kind:** self-flagged. **Brief exact quotation:** `"g","?"`; `Attention au \unsure{signe}`; `dès que $\unsure{\varphi}$ est iso`.  
**Context:** C84, E160.2 (scan 160), L174.2 (scan 174), on perfect duality and determinant twists. **Relation:** adds. **RESULTS_STATE:** RS-later; no direct entry for this sign check.

The map \(g:E\to\mathcal A\otimes L\) induces \(-\mathrm{id}_L\) on the submodule and the identity on the quotient, and the two elementary evaluations end in “c'est OK” and “mais c'est aussi OK.” The ensuing correspondence between \(Q\) and \(N\) is explicitly conditional on the displayed isomorphism, whose symbol is transcription-doubtful; it should not be promoted to an unconditional identification of every extension with an algebra. Compared with the later duality leads, this supplies an earlier, author-checked sign convention rather than a new general duality theorem.

## SA02-003 — MPL_84_097-145#3

**Heading:** A02 — The discriminant identity is asserted for a bare rank-two form

**Index kind:** unproved claim. **Brief exact quotation:** `Q_N(\varphi_N(x))=-N(x)\delta_N`; `sans autre donnée`.

**Scans cited:** 98,182

### A02 — The discriminant identity is asserted for a bare rank-two form

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan098.tex`, scan 098, approximately lines 7–19. **Kind:** unproved claim. **Brief exact quotation:** `Q_N(\varphi_N(x))=-N(x)\delta_N`; `sans autre donnée`.  
**Context:** C84, no exact match for `Q_N` or `\delta_N`; compare L182.3 (scan 182) only for the distinct distinction between abstract and induced pairings. **Relation:** adds. **RESULTS_STATE:** no direct entry.

The boxed formula is expressly claimed for any locally free rank-two module with a quadratic form, without additional algebra data. Its local \(Q\)-to-\(N\) comparison follows the minus sign on \(L\), but the minus sign in the displayed local norm is itself marked `\unsure{-}` and must remain a reading uncertainty. The statement therefore calls for reconstructing the meanings of \(Q_N,\varphi_N,\delta_N\) before checking it in that generality; the later product-discriminant formula is a different assertion and does not supply that proof.

## SA02-005 — MPL_84_097-145#5

**Heading:** A04 — A module transpose is claimed before quadratic algebra structure

**Index kind:** unproved claim. **Brief exact quotation:** `un sens sans utiliser les structures de rev.`; `$\chi=2$`.

### A04 — A module transpose is claimed before quadratic algebra structure

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan100.tex`, scan 100, approximately lines 16–32. **Kind:** unproved claim. **Brief exact quotation:** `un sens sans utiliser les structures de rev.`; `$\chi=2$`.  
**Context:** C84, E160.2 (scan 160), L174.2 (scan 174), and E170.2 (scan 170). **Relation:** adds. **RESULTS_STATE:** RS-χ.

The author claims that \(i_A:\mathcal A\to\mathcal A_1\otimes\mathcal A_2\) can be obtained as the transpose of the extension product without the covering forms or the specialization \(\chi=2\). This is a statement about the underlying dualized module construction; its use as an algebra homomorphism requires the later quadratic structure and compatibility checks. C84's perfect-pairing and coherence cautions apply precisely here: the existence of a linear transpose does not by itself establish the full categorical claim in RS-χ.

**Context:** C84, L180.1 (scan 180), on the rank-three boundary and rank-two pushout; E170.1 (scan 170), on exactness hypotheses. **Relation:** restates. **RESULTS_STATE:** RS-product, RS-χ.

The construction pushes the extension by \(\mathcal A_1\otimes_0\mathcal A_2\) out along the trace that restricts to the two input traces. Defining \(i_A\) then requires its proposed scalar map and tensor map to agree on this boundary, which is exactly the compatibility verified for \(1+\sigma_1\otimes\sigma_2\) on scan 102. The extension pushout alone is not yet a proof of a multiplication or of categorical coherence.

## SA02-007 — MPL_84_097-145#7

**Heading:** A06 — Scan 102 gives a conditional invariant-ring identification

**Index kind:** unproved claim. **Brief exact quotation:** `l'une des deux structures $E_1,E_2$ est étale`; `est univ injectif`.

**Scans cited:** 102,172,192

### A06 — Scan 102 gives a conditional invariant-ring identification

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan102.tex`, scan 102, approximately lines 23–36. **Kind:** unproved claim. **Brief exact quotation:** `l'une des deux structures $E_1,E_2$ est étale`; `est univ injectif`.  
**Context:** C84, L172.3–L172.5 (scan 172), L192.1 (scan 192). **Relation:** adds. **RESULTS_STATE:** RS-product, RS-later.

The NB assumes that at every characteristic-two point at least one input is étale, and under that hypothesis identifies the abstract product with the diagonal-involution invariants in the tensor algebra. In monic local coordinates the condition gives the covering by \(D(2),D(b_1),D(b_2)\); the invariant coefficient equations in `research/algebra_derivations.md` §2 prove equality of image and invariants on this cover, including after base change. This is stronger than the kernel calculation quoted in C84 L172.4 and is compatible with its exceptional cases, rather than a blanket claim of injectivity.

## SA02-008 — MPL_84_097-145#8

**Index kind:** self-flagged. **Brief exact quotation:** `serait, essentiellement, $z\bar z$`; `On devrait avoir`.

### A07 — The relative norm must lift through a possibly noninjective map

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan103.tex`, scan 103, approximately lines 14–40. **Kind:** self-flagged. **Brief exact quotation:** `serait, essentiellement, $z\bar z$`; `On devrait avoir`.  
**Context:** C84, L172.4–L172.5 (scan 172), and L190.2 (scan 190). **Relation:** adds. **RESULTS_STATE:** RS-product, RS-later.

The desired \(A\)-valued relative norm is stronger data than the tensor element \(z\bar z\), because a value in the image of \(A\to A_1\otimes A_2\) need not have a unique lift. The author therefore specifies its polarization through the already constructed relative trace and its four diagonal values \(1,c_1,c_2,c_1c_2\). C84 L190.2 gives the ordinary norm on a rank-two algebra, whereas the present problem is a quadratic map from the rank-four tensor algebra to its abstract rank-two product.

## SA02-011 — MPL_84_097-145#11

**Heading:** A10 — The relative trace factorization contains an essential factor two

**Index kind:** unpursued implication. **Brief exact quotation:** `i\bigl(\operatorname{Tr}_{A_1\otimes A_2/A}(\xi)\bigr)=\xi+\bar\xi`; `=2\eta`.

**Scans cited:** 105,172

### A10 — The relative trace factorization contains an essential factor two

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan105.tex`, scan 105, approximately lines 17–29. **Kind:** unpursued implication. **Brief exact quotation:** `i\bigl(\operatorname{Tr}_{A_1\otimes A_2/A}(\xi)\bigr)=\xi+\bar\xi`; `=2\eta`.  
**Context:** C84, L172.5 (scan 172). **Relation:** restates. **RESULTS_STATE:** RS-product, RS-later.

The two equations \(i\operatorname{Tr}=1+\sigma_1\otimes\sigma_2\) and \(\operatorname{Tr}i=2\mathrm{id}_A\) agree with the later lead, including the trace-basis values \((2,-b_1,-b_2,U+b_1b_2)\). Consequently the tensor operator squares to twice itself, and is a projector only after division by two is available. In particular, the identities alone neither split \(i\) over an arbitrary base nor imply its injectivity in characteristic two.

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan105.tex`, scan 105, approximately lines 36–39; `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan106.tex`, scan 106, lines 2–25. **Kind:** unproved claim. **Brief exact quotation:** `une application quadratique unique`; `\varphi_N(\xi,\eta)=\operatorname{Tr}_{A_1\otimes A_2/A}(\xi\bar\eta)`.  
**Context:** C84, L172.5 (scan 172), L190.2 (scan 190), with no relative rank-four-to-rank-two norm formula located. **Relation:** adds. **RESULTS_STATE:** RS-product.

For actual quadratic \(A_1,A_2\), the pure-tensor norm condition supplies the four diagonal values on a local tensor basis and the trace condition supplies its six mixed coefficients, so uniqueness does not require dividing by two. Existence still requires those coefficients to satisfy the claimed identities in the abstract target, rather than merely after applying \(i\). A12 supplies this local verification, including the scalar and multiplicative identities that turn the form into an \(A\)-quadratic relative norm.

## SA02-013 — MPL_84_097-145#13

**Heading:** A12 — A universal local relative norm completes the missing coefficients

**Index kind:** own idea. **Brief exact quotation:** `les éléments de la base $1,U_1,U_2,U_1\otimes U_2$`.

**Scans cited:** 103,105,172,190

### A12 — A universal local relative norm completes the missing coefficients

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan103.tex`, scan 103, lines 25–40; scans 105–107, norm proposition. **Kind:** own idea. **Brief exact quotation:** `les éléments de la base $1,U_1,U_2,U_1\otimes U_2$`.  
**Context:** C84, L172.1/L172.5 (scan 172), L190.2 (scan 190): the product relation and trace are present, but not this relative form. **Relation:** adds. **RESULTS_STATE:** RS-product, RS-later. **Confidence:** high for the local identities, from exact universal monic reduction.

For \(B=R[u,v]/(u^2+b_1u+c_1,v^2+b_2v+c_2)\) and \(A=R[t]/(t^2+b_1b_2t+C)\), \(C=b_1^2c_2+b_2^2c_1-4c_1c_2\), the norm on the ordered basis \((1,u,v,uv)\) has diagonal coefficients \((1,c_1,c_2,c_1c_2)\) and mixed coefficients \((-b_1,-b_2,t+b_1b_2,-t,-b_2c_1,-b_1c_2)\) in the order \(01,02,03,12,13,23\). The full polynomial in `research/algebra_derivations.md` §1 satisfies the pure-tensor condition, the required polarization, \(iN(\xi)=\xi\bar\xi\), multiplicativity, \(N(i(a))=a^2\), norm transitivity, and \(A\)-linearity of the relative trace. The seven checks in `scripts/research_relative_norm.py` reduce by monic relations over an integer polynomial ring without inverting a parameter; they establish these local algebraic identities and do not claim a new literature result or full categorical coherence.

**Heading:** A13 — Norm transitivity does not require cancelling the product embedding

**Scans cited:** 106,107,172,190

### A13 — Norm transitivity does not require cancelling the product embedding

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan106.tex`, scan 106, approximately lines 26–53; `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan107.tex`, scan 107, lines 2–12. **Kind:** self-flagged. **Brief exact quotation:** `Prouvons p.ex. la première`; `N\bar N=N_{A/O}(N)\qquad\text{OK}`.  
**Context:** C84, L172.4–L172.5 (scan 172), L190.2 (scan 190). **Relation:** adds. **RESULTS_STATE:** RS-product, RS-later.

The proposed proof groups the four conjugates into the two relative norms and ends with “OK,” but writes \(N(\xi)=\xi\bar\xi\) while suppressing the map \(i\). For actual rank-two input algebras, the final comparison is between scalars in \(R\), and \(R\to B\) is injective because \(B\) is locally free of rank four; this can justify the scalar equality without assuming \(A\to B\) injective. The abstract local norm calculation in A12 verifies the same transitivity directly, so the source's suppressed map need not be repaired in the transcript or used as an illicit cancellation.

## SA02-015 — MPL_84_097-145#15

**Heading:** A14 — The new axioms allow nonflat and nonfaithful scalar maps

**Index kind:** unpursued implication. **Brief exact quotation:** `ni même plat`; `soit injective`.

**Scans cited:** 107,108,172

### A14 — The new axioms allow nonflat and nonfaithful scalar maps

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan107.tex`, scan 107, approximately lines 16–32; scan 108, lines 6–9. **Kind:** unpursued implication. **Brief exact quotation:** `ni même plat`; `soit injective`.  
**Context:** C84, L172.4 (scan 172), on a noninjective map between free algebras, which is a different situation; no “quasi-quadratique” axiomatization located. **Relation:** differs. **RESULTS_STATE:** RS-product concerns actual quadratic algebras; no direct entry for the enlarged axioms.

Here \(k\to A\) itself may be noninjective and \(A\) need not be flat over \(k\), so the definition must not be silently replaced by the usual locally free rank-two notion. Nevertheless the displayed axioms already force every \(\lambda\in\ker(k\to A)\) to satisfy \(2\lambda=0\) and \(\lambda^2=0\), by applying \(t\) and \(n\) to \(\lambda1_A=0\). This elementary consequence locates the possible nonfaithfulness in two-torsion and is consistent with the \(\mathbf Z/4\) example in A20.

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan108.tex`, scan 108, approximately lines 48–69. **Kind:** withdrawn in same text. **Brief exact quotation:** `\deleted{\unsure{3^\circ)}}`; `donc on a bien $n(\bar x)=n(x)$`.

The cancelled label does not cancel the conclusion: the author derives \(n(\bar x)=n(x)\) from \(\bar x=t(x)1-x\), the quadratic translation formula, and \(n(1)=1\). The two \(t(x)^2\) terms cancel in the target \(k\), so this proof does not need injectivity of \(k\to A\) or multiplicativity of \(n\). This completed check should be distinguished from the genuinely missing multiplicativity condition in the later recap.

## SA02-017 — MPL_84_097-145#17

**Heading:** A16 — When two is invertible the form reconstructs multiplication

**Index kind:** unpursued implication. **Brief exact quotation:** `Au facteur $2$ près`; `la structure d'algèbre de $A$ est connue`.

**Scans cited:** 108,109,148,172

### A16 — When two is invertible the form reconstructs multiplication

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan108.tex`, scan 108, approximately lines 33–46; scan 109, lines 18–32. **Kind:** unpursued implication. **Brief exact quotation:** `Au facteur $2$ près`; `la structure d'algèbre de $A$ est connue`.  
**Context:** C84, E148.4 (scan 148), L172.5 (scan 172), for distinct warnings about multiplicativity and division by two. **Relation:** adds. **RESULTS_STATE:** no direct entry for this reconstruction.

Combining multiplicativity of the conjugation with the subsequently imposed polarization identity gives \(2xy=t(x)y+t(y)x-\alpha(\varphi_n(x,y))\), whose right side is known from the pointed module, \(t\), and \(n\). Thus the multiplication is determined by those data when two is invertible, while the displayed equation cannot recover an arbitrary two-torsion contribution otherwise. In the same invertible-two case \(t(\lambda1)=2\lambda\) forces \(\alpha\) injective, explaining why the nonfaithful phenomenon in A20 cannot occur there.

### A19 — The five-axiom recap omits the earlier multiplicativity condition

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan108.tex`, scan 108, lines 4–9 and 46; `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan110.tex`, scan 110, lines 9–42. **Kind:** reading question. **Brief exact quotation:** `n(xy)=n(x)n(y)`; `satisfaisant $1^\circ$ à $5^\circ$`.  
**Context:** C84, E148.4 (scan 148), a related but different multiplicativity warning; exact searches for “quasi-quadratique” and this five-axiom issue are negative. **Relation:** adds. **RESULTS_STATE:** RS-product concerns actual quadratic algebras and does not settle this axiom question.

Condition (b) on scan 108 requires \(n\) to be multiplicative, but the five numbered conditions used to define “quasi-quadratique” on scan 110 do not repeat it. The margin on scan 108 explains why multiplicativity follows when \(\alpha\) is injective; A20 proves that it is not redundant for the explicitly allowed noninjective maps. Hence the displayed five axioms strictly weaken the earlier list containing (b), without deciding whether the author intended a weaker definition or an abbreviated recap.

## SA02-021 — MPL_84_097-145#21

**Heading:** A20 — A finite counterexample separates the two axiom lists

**Index kind:** own idea. **Brief exact quotation:** `algèbre \uline{quasi-quadratique}`.

**Scans cited:** 108,110,148

### A20 — A finite counterexample separates the two axiom lists

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan110.tex`, scan 110, approximately lines 9–42; compare scan 108, line 6. **Kind:** own idea. **Brief exact quotation:** `algèbre \uline{quasi-quadratique}`.  
**Context:** C84, no matching weak-axiom example; E148.4 (scan 148) concerns a different implication. **Relation:** adds. **RESULTS_STATE:** no direct entry; RS-product is about actual quadratic algebras. **Confidence:** high, from the coefficient proof and exhaustive finite check.

Let \(k=\mathbf Z/4\), \(A=\mathbf F_2[e,f]/(e^2,f^2)\), and for \(x=a+be+cf+d\,ef\) with binary coefficients set \(t(x)=2a\) and \(n(x)=a+2d\), using the reduction scalar map. Then \(\bar x=x\), the polarization is \(2aa'\), and all five displayed axioms hold, whereas \(n(ef)=2\ne n(e)n(f)=0\). Multiplicativity fails even on units: \(n((1+e)(1+f))=3\) while \(n(1+e)n(1+f)=1\). The proof in `research/algebra_derivations.md` §3 extends the construction to quadratic laws under every \(k\)-algebra base change, and `scripts/research_algebra_checks.py` checks all four scalars, sixteen algebra elements, and 256 ordered pairs, finding 96 multiplicativity failures.

Take \(k=\mathbf F_2\), \(A=k[e,f]/(e^2,f^2)\), and \(A_1,A_2,A_3\) generated respectively by \(e,f,e+f\); each pair tensor map is an isomorphism and \(A\) is genuinely quadratic over each \(A_i\). For three copies of \(k[z]/(z^2)\) over \(k=\mathbf F_2\), the contracted algebra is \(B_0=k[t]/(t^2)\) and the full tensor algebra is \(B=k[e,f,g]/(e^2,f^2,g^2)\), with \(t\mapsto0\). The map on spectra is finite and surjective, but tensoring the injection \((t)\hookrightarrow B_0\) with \(B\) gives the zero map from \((t)\otimes_{B_0}B\simeq B\ne0\), proving nonflatness. Its fibre at \(t=0\) has \(k\)-dimension eight, demonstrating why the indicated degree four is not a constant flat rank. The derivation and finite-algebra check illustrate the author's warning and do not disprove the weaker quasi-quadratic program of A37.

**Index kind:** own idea; **Confidence:** high under the stated exact-sequence hypotheses, because the two cancellations are by a monomorphism and an epimorphism; **Context relation:** adds a precise proof to the later identities reported in C E170.3, scan 170, rather than proving RS-Hχ as a whole; **Register:** RS-Hχ.

### B02 — A proof of the questioned equivalence needs exactness, not division

**Source:** pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan124.tex, cote 84, scan 124, lines 2–11; **Kind:** own idea; **Confidence:** high under the stated exact-sequence hypotheses, because the two cancellations are by a monomorphism and an epimorphism; **Context relation:** adds a precise proof to the later identities reported in C E170.3, scan 170, rather than proving RS-Hχ as a whole; **Register:** RS-Hχ.

For short exact rows with inclusion maps \(a,a'\), quotient maps \(q,q'\), and a row morphism \((r,f,s)\), assume the complementary identities \(a\pi+\omega q=\chi I\) and \(a'\pi'+\omega'q'=\chi I\). Then \(r\pi=\pi'f\) implies \((f\omega-\omega's)q=0\), hence \(f\omega=\omega's\), while the latter equality implies \(a'(r\pi-\pi'f)=0\), hence the former. This supplies a conditional answer to the manuscript's “?” without cancelling \(\chi\), and works even when \(\chi\) is a zero divisor. It does not repair the doubtful letters or the incomplete calculation on the sheet.

No division by \(2\) occurs, so this is not an eigenspace decomposition assertion, including when the two endpoint signs coincide in characteristic \(2\).

The opening names endomorphisms \(f_i\) but freezes the restriction as \((u_i|f_i)(L_i)\subset L_i\), so \(f_i(L_i)\subset L_i\) is the apparent intended hypothesis, not a certain reading of that letter; the sheet then proposes \(f(x_1*x_2)=f_1(x_1)*f_2(x_2)\) and the endpoint map \(f^!_1\otimes f^!_2\). Under that interpretation, its mixed-summand diagram also needs the equality \(\pi_1f_1=f^!_1\pi_1\), and likewise for the second factor, whereas preservation of \(L_i\) alone does not supply it. The unfinished “Ex” and following incomplete equations therefore remain a live compatibility investigation, not a proof of functoriality for every submodule-preserving endomorphism.

### B24 — A shear detects the missing compatibility on scan 144

**Source:** pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan144.tex, cote 84, scan 144, lines 12–37; **Kind:** own idea; **Confidence:** high for the stated split vector-space example, because the defining relation is tested by explicit matrices; **Context relation:** adds a counterexample to the broader proposed hypotheses while leaving C R11's restricted morphism model intact; **Register:** RS-Hχ.

Under B23's interpretation of the initial hypothesis, take \(\chi=1\), \(E_i=k\ell_i\oplus km_i\), \(L_i=k\ell_i\), \(\pi_i(\ell_i)=\ell_i\), \(\pi_i(m_i)=0\), \(f_2=\mathrm{id}\), and the submodule-preserving shear \(f_1(\ell_1)=\ell_1,\ f_1(m_1)=m_1+\ell_1\). The product relation gives \(m_1*\ell_2=0\), whereas the proposed image is \((m_1+\ell_1)*\ell_2=\ell_1\otimes\ell_2\ne0\). The calculation report verifies the same obstruction with the tensor quotient matrix, so the displayed action cannot descend under submodule preservation alone. This does not refute functoriality for morphisms satisfying \(\pi_if_i=f_i^!\pi_i\).

## SA02-071 — MPL_84_097-145#71

**Heading:** C04 — A precise comparison target, with its hypotheses

**Index kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** medium as a future search direction, low as an account of the author's intention, because the required rank-three or projective datum is absent. **Context:** `README.md`, B12; `same_cote_84/MPL_84_146-193_notes.md`, R03, scans 160, 192; `notes/RESULTS_STATE.md`, **MPL_84_146-193 §2**.

**Scans cited:** 98,104,160

### C04 — A precise comparison target, with its hypotheses

**Source:** cote 84, `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan098.tex`, boxed quadratic-form identity; scan 104, algebra presentation. **Kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** medium as a future search direction, low as an account of the author's intention, because the required rank-three or projective datum is absent. **Context:** `README.md`, B12; `same_cote_84/MPL_84_146-193_notes.md`, R03, scans 160, 192; `notes/RESULTS_STATE.md`, **MPL_84_146-193 §2**.

For a comparison outside these pages, Voight's *Quaternion algebras*, Corollaries 5.2.6 and 5.5.2, gives the correspondence between quaternion algebras, smooth conics and similarity classes of nondegenerate ternary forms over fields of characteristic different from two, and Theorem 5.6.8 with Corollary 5.6.12 identifies the corresponding automorphism group through the special orthogonal group. In the split quaternion case the quotient of units by scalar units is PGL₂, giving a precise field-level meaning to the planning shorthand SO(3) ≃ GP(1); this source does not authorize dropping its hypotheses in the manuscript's characteristic-two calculations. This **restates** R03's search direction with an independently opened primary reference, and supplies no claim that Grothendieck uses that construction here.

## D. Research items from the reused typed leaves

These items record the author's claims, qualifications and recollections as archival evidence. His psychological and cultural generalizations retain the status of attributed claims. The common source-file prefix is `pages/MPL_84_097-145_84_scan`, with the complete filename given in every item.

### E.3. Qualifications and withdrawals in the reused typed leaves

These are the author's limits on predictions, interpretations and recollections, rather than unfinished algebraic proofs. The linked D items contain their individual context comparisons and searches. A doubtful letter or clipped ending inside `\unsure` is editorial uncertainty and does not itself count as one of the author's reservations.

| Scan and frozen TeX line(s) | Brief author wording | What is qualified or left open | Research item |
|---|---|---|---|
| 125:3,8–11 | “Il me semble”; “probable”; “peut-être” | Characterization of the epoch, forecast of catastrophe, and its proposed consequence. The broken word before “probable” remains doubtful in the transcription. | D01 |
| 125:30 | “Il semblerait” | Generalization about childhood mechanisms determining later life. | D03 |
| 127:3–4 | “raisons qui restent encore mystérieuses pour moi” | Reason for the different course of his own inner history. | D04 |
| 127:29–30 | “mystère” | Early flourishing despite the environment he describes. | D05 |
| 129:18 | “je ne suis pas plus convaincu que lui” | Validity of the standard conjectures; the claim about their significance is separate. | D07 |
| 131:16,28,30 | “Je ne savais sans doute pas”; “je crois” (twice) | Retrospective access to childhood awareness and the extent of disagreement with his father. | D09 |
| 133:15–16 | “que j'ai cru pouvoir situer à quelques mois près”; “se placerait” | Approximate dating of the remembered inward rupture. | D10 |
| 133:35 | “Peut-être le mystère n'est plus” | Tentative relocation of the explanatory puzzle. | D11 |
| 135:26,30,36–40 | “Il me semble”; “peut-être toujours”; “J'ai tendance à croire”; “ne saurais aujourd'hui encore dire” | Proposed universal balance, existence and persistence of a dominant note, and inability to identify his own. The parenthetic “il me semble” and “jamais peut-être” also limit the retrospective claim and possible future manifestation. | D13 |
| 137:7–14 | “il se pourrait”; “Ce n'est pas le lieu ici de passer en revue” | Proposed predominance of the creative feminine and the explicitly deferred supporting review. | D14 |
| 137:24–25,41 | “je crois”; frozen `peut-êtr|peut-être`; “surtout je crois” | Proposed explanation of childhood appearances, the long hair, and the rarity of boys' doll play. The clipped “peut-être” is also an editorial reading doubt. | D15 |
| 143:6 | “Contrairement à ce dont je croyais hâtivement me souvenir tantôt” | Explicit withdrawal of a remembered order of events; both the abandoned recollection and correction are stated on this same scan. | D19 |
| 143:12 | “il me semble” | Degree to which the intuition subsequently receded from attention. | D21 |
| 145:4 | “plusieurs années, je crois” | Duration before the described attitudes were internalized. | D17 |
| 145:6 | “jusqu'à présent”; “Je crois pouvoir” | Provisional two-force enumeration and claimed sudden unconscious choice; the following word “dire” is transcribed doubtfully. | D22–D23 |

The absence of an authorial question mark on another claim does not certify it: the itemized notes separately record unproved assertions. Conversely, the quoted “OK” in a mathematical check records the author's local conclusion, and the standing mathematical tables preserve where that conclusion is narrower than a proposed general claim.

## G. Tables and enumerations

## SA02-105 — MPL_84_097-145#105

**Heading:** Scope and counting rules

**Index kind:** 

**Scans cited:** 

### Scope and counting rules

Every explicit finite list, labelled condition system, table/comparison array, selected symbolic cases, typed explicit enumeration, and archival count found in the 48 frozen page texts. Ordinary derivation lines and unnumbered rhetorical contrasts are not automatically sets. No images or OCR used by this cataloger.

Author-stated numerals, counted written entries/layout cells, and independently derived model totals are distinguished in each counts field. Reading uncertainty is separate from cardinality.

The rank-four tensor algebra in the quadratic calculations is commutative. Neither that rank nor the finite four-point model establishes the rank-four central Azumaya algebra required by the planning bridge B12. No regular-polyhedron/congruence-level enumeration is found here; the unrelated LM2 claims are not advanced by these counts.

Context shorthand below refers throughout to `context/same_cote_84/MPL_84_146-193_notes.md`. Those earlier notes are first-pass leads, including their reported calculations and pagination. The cited Hua entries in `context/notes/RESULTS_STATE.md` are AI-produced claims, not reviewed results.

Machine-readable entries, exact source basenames/anchor lines, and search terms are in `research/enumerations.json`; the flat companion is `research/enumerations.csv`. Run `python3 scripts/recompute_enumerations.py` for the declared finite models and symbolic checks; its output distinguishes verified models from unproved manuscript assertions.

## SA02-112 — MPL_84_097-145#112

**Heading:** H.1. Components of the extension-product claim

**Index kind:** 

**Scans cited:** 128,138,142,144

### H.1. Components of the extension-product claim

| Clause in Hua findings register 84-chi-trivialised-extension-product | Evidence in the eleven handwritten pages | What remains outside that evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Local product coefficient \(b=b_1\otimes b_2\) | Explicit on scans 128 and 130; splitting-change formula on 130–134. | A consistent choice of every draft sign and transport convention, followed by the required descent proof. |
| Product for every \(\chi\) | The product formulas themselves do not require dividing by \(\chi\); their local cocycle has a positive-convention polynomial model. | A \(\gamma\chi=2\) condition on a later comparison cannot be silently imposed on the product itself; the bare \(\chi^{-1}\) notation on scan 138 also cannot supply a global hypothesis. |
| Functoriality | Isomorphisms and homomorphisms asserted on 126; compatible mixed-summand actions investigated on 124, 138 and 144. | Compatibility of morphisms with the chosen \(\chi\)-splittings; scan 144's initial submodule-preserving hypothesis is insufficient. |
| Pushout/pullback agreement | Desired strict-endpoint comparison, \(\gamma\chi=2\), and a candidate middle block on 128/142. | Existence in other regimes, endpoint sign conventions, actual descent and naturality of the candidate; comparison is not settled merely by conditional block data. |
| Symmetric monoidal category | Local multiplication and an attempted binary-change cocycle. | Unit, associator, symmetry and coherence, plus identification of C R11's local model with the claimed global category. |
| \(M=\mathcal O\) invertibility iff \(T_0\) generates \(L\) on \(V(\chi)\) | No occurrence of that criterion in these eleven pages. | C R12's local ideal criterion and its connection to Hua's particular \(T_0\), as well as any global claim. |
| \(\chi=2\) involution language | Explicit on scan 142, with a direct exact-sequence interpretation in B22. | Invertibility of \(2\), eigenspace splitting, and stronger algebra/trace identifications are not consequences. |

These are scope distinctions for the handwritten extension block only. They do not classify the intervening typed leaves or use their contents as extension-theoretic evidence.

## I. Reference and verification limits

**Opened primary comparison source.** John Voight, *Quaternion Algebras* (2021), chapter 5, “Ternary quadratic forms and quaternion algebras,” pp. 65–84, DOI [10.1007/978-3-030-56694-4_5](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-56694-4_5): Corollaries 5.2.6 and 5.5.2, Theorem 5.6.8 and Corollary 5.6.12, used only in C04 with their field and characteristic hypotheses. The mathematical formula checks elsewhere in these notes are explicit calculations in the included scripts, not deductions from a literature citation.

The context's references to Voight (2016), Loos (1996), Wood (2011) and other literature remain references reported by that context unless an individual note expressly says otherwise. No published version of the manuscript was used to supply or change a reading. These notes make no priority or novelty claim, and local calculations are not promoted to global categorical coherence statements.

The combined transcription and its 48 page files were frozen before the context contents were read. Later reading questions identify a source line and a contextual alternative, and leave the frozen text unchanged. Line numbers in these research items locate the individual frozen TeX file; paragraph and footnote descriptions locate the source page where physical line numbers would otherwise be misleading after reflow.

## SA02-113 — MPL_84_146-193#1

**Heading:** Principal findings and their exact limits

**Index kind:** 

**Scans cited:** 146,147,148,154,162,168,170,172

### Principal findings and their exact limits

- **A precise local algebraic result:** scans 172 and 192 give a quadratic-algebra product whose relation and discriminant formula check over the integer polynomial ring; its local formula matches Voight (2016, Construction 3.14) after an explicit generator shift (L172.1–L172.2, R05).
- **An abstract product can survive a failed tensor realization:** the image map has kernel \(\operatorname{Ann}_R(2,b_1,b_2)\,t\) in the declared monic local model, refining the manuscript's characteristic-two warning and producing a concrete \(\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z\) example (L172.3–L172.6, L192.1).
- **A positive all-χ local model:** the rank-one coordinate groupoid admits the multiplication \(a\star b=a\sigma+\tau b+\chi ab\), and an object is invertible precisely when \((\tau,\chi)=R\); this supports local parts of Hua's claim but does not prove its full global identification (R11–R13).
- **A separate sign obstruction:** for the explicitly fixed endpoint conventions, the pushout and pullback extension cocycles are opposite; a χ=0 elliptic-curve example prevents an isomorphism fixing both ends (E170.4–E170.5). This does not refute a sign-twisted interpretation or the whole ellipsized Hua entry.
- **No explicit B12 construction in the supplied interval:** no form of P¹, conic, rank-four central Azumaya algebra, or ternary-form/SO(3) identification is constructed here (R01–R04). The folder plan's B5 regular-polyhedron/congruence questions likewise receive no demonstrated step (R09).
- **Concrete archival counts and revisions:** the coefficient tables on scans 154 and 164 have 16 and 12 data cells respectively; the typed lists give \(6+6+2=14\) pairs and \(2^6=64\) sign sequences, while the eighteen groups and eighteen meditation leaves are separately reported counts (table registers below).

The packet contains 24 handwritten mathematical pages (even scans 146–192) alternating with 24 fully transcribed reused typescript pages (odd scans 147–193). The mathematical sequence concerns extensions, χ-trivialisations, duality, tensor comparison maps and quadratic algebras of rank two; the reused text concerns the author's reflective treatment of yin/yang, parental figures, creativity and later revisions of that treatment. Its dates belong to the reused text and cannot by themselves date the mathematical writing on the other sides. Archival pencil numbers 145–192, original typescript pagination, footnote numbers, and dates are separate numbering systems.

The supplied context consists of only `README.md`, `LM2_CONGRUENCE_ROWS.md`, and `notes/RESULTS_STATE.md`; the larger files named inside the last register were not supplied. A register excerpt can establish what an earlier run claimed and what review status was reported, but cannot substitute for examining its omitted proof. In particular, the claim status of each LM2/LM4/LM7 entry remains as stated in that register; no such theorem is upgraded by these notes.

## 2. Supplied context and the audit of its direct claim

Each numbered item has a context comparison. The actual searches, including negative and incidental lexical matches, are preserved in `records/context_queries_early.json`, `context_queries_late.json`, `context_queries_typed.json`, and `context_queries_root.json`. The searches cover all three supplied context files; they do not purport to search the absent source files merely named in the results register.

The principal direct entry is **Hua, findings register, 84-chi-trivialised-extension-product**, reproduced in `notes/RESULTS_STATE.md` under “Findings of another project.” Its status is an AI-produced conjectural candidate, with an intake saying that this cote's package was still awaited, not a mathematical referee verdict. The aggregate interval attached to it includes reused typed leaves, but those leaves do not contain the extension-product assertion; other cited mathematical ranges are outside this packet. The following verdicts are consequently restricted to the evidence supplied here and the explicitly identified local calculations.

| Component of Hua, findings register, **84-chi-trivialised-extension-product** | Evidence in the supplied interval | Assessment |
|---|---|---|
| Extensions with a fixed \(\chi\)-splitting | Scan 170 gives the extension diagrams and identities; scans 146–168 calculate with them. | Supported as the intended objects, subject to the written universal-injectivity and local-freeness hypotheses and the frozen sign doubt. |
| Product by amalgamated sum without division | Scans 168 and 170 explicitly mark the relevant squares “cocart”; their structural maps use \(\pi_i\), not division by \(\chi\). | Supported as a construction plan; tensor exactness and the resulting splitting data still need proof in the stated category. |
| \(b=b_1\otimes b_2\) in split coordinates | Scan 146 circles \(b=b_1b_2\) and writes coordinate changes. | Direct local evidence; global descent is not proved by the circled formula alone. |
| Symmetric monoidal category for every \(\chi\) | No associativity, unit, symmetry, or coherence data are written in these mathematical scans. | R11 independently verifies a strict symmetric monoidal groupoid for fixed trivial rank-one endpoints over a commutative ring, for every \(\chi\), with automorphisms retained as in R13; global descent and identification with the manuscript's category remain unverified, and other ranges cited by Hua were not supplied. |
| Invertible iff \(T_0\) generates \(L\) on \(V(\chi)\) for \(M=\mathcal O\) | No \(T_0\), \(V(\chi)\), or inverse criterion appears here. | R12 verifies the precise local-model criterion \((\tau,\chi)=R\), equivalently that \(\tau\bmod\chi\) is a unit, strengthening E146.3's residue observation; identification with the named global section \(T_0\), varying line bundles, and the omitted manuscript passages remains conditional. |
| Pushout and pullback products agree | Scans 162 and 168 mark comparison arrows “?”; scan 170 asserts a canonical application; scans 148–158 expose \(\gamma\chi=2\) or \(\delta\chi=2\). | Supported as an authorial goal/claim; the fixed-endpoint all-\(\chi\) interpretation has the conditional obstruction in E170.4, and sign conventions or restrictions must be checked in the omitted ranges. |
| Novelty or absence of literature | Nothing on these scans establishes a literature search. | No novelty conclusion is adopted. |

## SA02-115 — MPL_84_146-193#3

### E146.1 — The multiplicative coefficient is a local assertion

**File/scan/lines:** `MPL_84_146-193_84_scan146.tex`, 146, 13 and 31–37. **Kind:** unproved claim. **Context comparison:** adds explicit local evidence to Hua, findings register, **84-chi-trivialised-extension-product**; no matching settled context entry.

The circled “\(b=b_1b_2\)” is accompanied by the changes “`b'_1=b_1+\unsure{\chi|\gamma}u_1`” and “`b'_2=b_2+\unsure{\chi|\gamma}u_2`,” so a proof must check independence of splittings rather than simply multiply two chosen coordinates. This supports the local coefficient part of Hua’s claim, while the tensor interpretation requires the indicated Hom domains and the surviving doubt over the scalar to be respected. It does not itself construct associativity, a unit, or an inverse.

## SA02-116 — MPL_84_146-193#4

**Heading:** E146.2 — The two displayed matrices do not require division by two

**Index kind:** unpursued implication. **Context comparison:** adds a concrete integral calculation to Hua, **84-chi-trivialised-extension-product**; no matrix identity with these entries was found elsewhere in the supplied context.

### E146.2 — The two displayed matrices do not require division by two

**File/scan/lines:** `MPL_84_146-193_84_scan146.tex`, 146, 5–9. **Kind:** unpursued implication. **Context comparison:** adds a concrete integral calculation to Hua, **84-chi-trivialised-extension-product**; no matrix identity with these entries was found elsewhere in the supplied context.

For the two displayed matrices “\(\begin{pmatrix}2&0\\-b_1&0\end{pmatrix}\)” and “\(\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\b_1&2\end{pmatrix}\),” direct multiplication gives sum \(2I\), both cross-products zero, and squares equal to twice the respective matrix. These are integral identities, but replacing either matrix by half of itself to obtain an idempotent would require an additional hypothesis. `early_formula_checks.py` verifies the four identities without deciding the doubtful names of the operators.

## SA02-117 — MPL_84_146-193#5

**Heading:** E146.3 — A residue class is a useful, limited invariant

**Index kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** high for the conditional algebraic observation, because the polynomial identity is checked exactly; no claim of novelty. **Context comparison:** refines only the local coefficient clause of Hua, **84-chi-trivialised-extension-product**.

**Scans cited:** 146

### E146.3 — A residue class is a useful, limited invariant

**File/scan/lines:** `MPL_84_146-193_84_scan146.tex`, 146, 31–37. **Kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** high for the conditional algebraic observation, because the polynomial identity is checked exactly; no claim of novelty. **Context comparison:** refines only the local coefficient clause of Hua, **84-chi-trivialised-extension-product**.

If the scalar in “`b'_i=b_i+\unsure{\chi|\gamma}u_i`” is \(\chi\), then \(b'_1b'_2-b_1b_2=\chi(u_1b_2+b_1u_2+\chi u_1u_2)\), so the product is independent of this change modulo \(\chi\). This suggests separating a residue invariant on \(V(\chi)\) from a globally defined product extension and its coherence data. The computation is compatible with the locus occurring in Hua’s invertibility statement, but neither a section called \(T_0\) nor that invertibility criterion occurs in this interval.

## SA02-118 — MPL_84_146-193#6

**Heading:** E148.1 — The involution ansatz imposes a scalar condition

### E148.1 — The involution ansatz imposes a scalar condition

**File/scan/lines:** `MPL_84_146-193_84_scan148.tex`, 148, 5–23. **Kind:** unproved claim. **Context comparison:** qualifies the scope of the local evidence for Hua, **84-chi-trivialised-extension-product**.

The substitutions “\(\sigma_i=\mathrm{id}_i-\gamma\pi_i\)” lead to the boxed “\(\gamma\chi=2\),” with \(\beta=-1\) and \(\alpha=\chi\). This is a condition on this particular representation of the comparison operator and cannot be treated as a universally available choice over an arbitrary base. Conversely, failure to choose such a \(\gamma\) would not by itself disprove a different all-\(\chi\) construction of the kind claimed in Hua’s register.

The symbolic check records the implication, not a correction of the author’s formulas.

## SA02-123 — MPL_84_146-193#11

**Heading:** E150.2 — The displayed double-conjugation check specializes to two

**Index kind:** unproved claim with a displayed local check. **Context comparison:** supports the \(\chi=2\) part of an extension comparison, not the unrestricted quantifier in Hua, **84-chi-trivialised-extension-product**.

### E150.2 — The displayed double-conjugation check specializes to two

**File/scan/lines:** `MPL_84_146-193_84_scan150.tex`, 150, 16–29. **Kind:** unproved claim with a displayed local check. **Context comparison:** supports the \(\chi=2\) part of an extension comparison, not the unrestricted quantifier in Hua, **84-chi-trivialised-extension-product**.

The calculation ending “\(=x_1\)” explicitly places \(2\) beneath \(\chi\), while the page also writes “\(\pi(\pi-\chi)=0\).” Under that latter relation, the map \(s=1-\pi\) satisfies \(s^2-1=(\chi-2)\pi\), so involutivity follows at \(\chi=2\) but need not hold for arbitrary \(\chi\). This explains why the scalar hypothesis must remain visible in any interpretation of the norm-like tensor formula.

**File/scan/lines:** `MPL_84_146-193_84_scan152.tex`, 152, 15–16. **Kind:** withdrawn in the same text / self-check completed. **Context comparison:** supplies an explicit internal correction not reported in Hua, **84-chi-trivialised-extension-product**.

The author cancels “\(2\chi=\chi\)” and writes “\(\chi=\chi\quad\text{o.K.!}\).” The preceding polynomial \(-\chi(1-\gamma\chi)+2(1-\gamma\chi)\chi+\gamma\chi^2\) is indeed identically \(\chi\), as the accompanying script checks. This verifies that scalar restriction only, not every compatibility of the full extension map.

## SA02-151 — MPL_84_146-193#39

**Heading:** E170.4 — A conditional counterexample distinguishes equality from a sign twist

**Index kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** high for the explicitly specified fixed-endpoint interpretation, because the transition matrices and scalar residuals are computed; no claim of novelty or blanket refutation of Hua. **Context comparison:** conditionally contradicts the statement that the pushout and pullback products “agree” in Hua, **84-chi-trivialised-extension-product**, if that means an isomorphism inducing identities on both ends for every \(\chi\).

**Scans cited:** 166,168,170

### E170.4 — A conditional counterexample distinguishes equality from a sign twist

**File/scan/lines:** `MPL_84_146-193_84_scan168.tex`, 168, 9–13; `MPL_84_146-193_84_scan170.tex`, 170, 19–36; compare `MPL_84_146-193_84_scan166.tex`, 166, 19–28. **Kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** high for the explicitly specified fixed-endpoint interpretation, because the transition matrices and scalar residuals are computed; no claim of novelty or blanket refutation of Hua. **Context comparison:** conditionally contradicts the statement that the pushout and pullback products “agree” in Hua, **84-chi-trivialised-extension-product**, if that means an isomorphism inducing identities on both ends for every \(\chi\).

At \(\chi=0\), take \(L_i=M_i=\mathcal O_X\), \(\pi_i=\psi_i\), and \(\varpi_i=-\alpha_i\); the diagrams then give the Baer sum of the two extension classes for the pushout product and its negative for the pullback product. For a nonzero class \(e\in H^1(X,\mathcal O_X)\) on a smooth elliptic curve over a characteristic-zero field and a split second extension, these classes are \(e\) and \(-e\), which differ because \(2e\ne0\) (The Stacks Project Authors, n.d., Tags 0B3A and 072J). Therefore no comparison fixing both ends exists in this example, although a comparison negating the subobject does exist, so an unrecorded sign convention or restriction to \(\chi=2\) can change the verdict. The derivation below separates this precise obstruction from the existence of a product operation or a symmetric-monoidal structure, neither of which the example refutes.

### E170.5 — Canonicity requires a compatible choice across splitting changes

**File/scan/lines:** `MPL_84_146-193_84_scan146.tex`, 146, 31–37; `MPL_84_146-193_84_scan154.tex`, 154, 43–45; `MPL_84_146-193_84_scan170.tex`, 170, 19–21. **Kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** high within the explicit local model, because the change-of-basis identity is verified symbolically; no claim of novelty. **Context comparison:** refines the canonicity issue in Hua, **84-chi-trivialised-extension-product**.

Writing a local endpoint-preserving comparison as \((l,m)\mapsto(l+k(m),m)\), a change of splitting by \((u_1,u_2)\) requires \(k'-k=2t\), where \(t=u_1\tau_2+\tau_1u_2+\chi u_1u_2\). The candidate \(k=\delta\tau_1\tau_2\) changes by \(\delta\chi t\), so its gluing defect is \((\delta\chi-2)t\). This explains simultaneously the residual Hom freedom on scan 154 and the condition \(\delta\chi=2\) on scans 156–158, without promoting a choice of local matrix to a canonical global construction.

**Brief quotation:** `A\hookrightarrow A_1\otimes A_2`

For the displayed local model, \(i:R[t]/(t^2+bt+c)\to B=A_1\otimes_R A_2\), \(t\mapsto2u_1u_2+b_2u_1+b_1u_2\), has kernel \(\operatorname{Ann}_R(2,b_1,b_2)\,t\), because the four tensor-basis coefficients of \(i(a+dt)\) are \((a,db_2,db_1,2d)\). Thus \(R=\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z\), \(b_1=b_2=c_1=c_2=0\), gives the nonzero kernel element \(2t\), while a split injection requires the stronger condition \((2,b_1,b_2)=R\). Confidence: high for this conditional algebraic statement, since it is a direct coefficient calculation and the script independently enumerates the small-ring examples.

## SA02-185 — MPL_84_146-193#73

**Heading:** L190.2

**Index kind:** unproved claim.

**Scans cited:** 146,190,198

### L190.2

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan190.tex`, scan 190, approximate TeX lines 10–12. **Kind:** unproved claim.

**Brief quotation:** `\mathrm N_{A/k}(u_1*u_2)=\lambda^2c+\mu^2\unsure{-|+}\lambda\mu\beta`

For \(u^2+\beta u+c=0\), the exact formulas are \(\operatorname{Tr}(\lambda u+\mu)=-\lambda\beta+2\mu\) and \(\operatorname N(\lambda u+\mu)=\lambda^2c+\mu^2-\lambda\mu\beta\). The script verifies both from the multiplication matrix; this favors the minus alternative mathematically but does not change the frozen uncertain sign.

**Context comparison:** `context/notes/RESULTS_STATE.md`, Hua, findings register, 84-chi-trivialised-extension-product (conjectural, unreviewed; cited scans 146–170) contains no trace/norm calculation; LM2 C.d2 in `context/LM2_CONGRUENCE_ROWS.md` concerns different matrix-norm equations at cote 140-3 scans 198 and 202–203 and does not settle this local quadratic identity.

## SA02-186 — MPL_84_146-193#74

**Heading:** L190.3

**Scans cited:** 146,176,190

### L190.3

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan190.tex`, scan 190, approximate TeX lines 10, 25–27; compare scan 176, 19–23. **Kind:** reading question.

**Brief quotation:** `u_1*u_2=\lambda u+\mu`

The scalar names are reversed relative to scan 176: there \(\lambda\) is the constant coefficient and \(\mu\) the coefficient of \(u\), while here they occur as \(\lambda u+\mu\). This is a change of local notation, so carrying the earlier numerical values into the later names without swapping them would create a false inconsistency.

**Context comparison:** `context/notes/RESULTS_STATE.md`, Hua, findings register, 84-chi-trivialised-extension-product (conjectural, unreviewed; cited scans 146–170) gives no λ/μ convention; this cross-page comparison adds a notation warning while leaving both frozen readings intact.

## SA02-188 — MPL_84_146-193#76

**Heading:** L192.1

**Index kind:** self-flagged: a step he himself marks as unverified.

**Scans cited:** 146,192

### L192.1

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan192.tex`, scan 192, approximate TeX lines 8–12. **Kind:** self-flagged: a step he himself marks as unverified.

**Brief quotation:** `$A$: avec $1$ \unsure{ça} \unsure{croule} si $2=0,\ b_1=b_2=0$`

At that specialization the displayed tensor element \(u_1*u_2\) is zero, so together with one it cannot be a basis of an embedded rank-two algebra. The abstract monic quadratic algebra nevertheless remains free of rank two, and in characteristic two with \(b_1=b_2=1\) the construction instead gives \(t^2+t+c_1+c_2\), preserving the noncollapsed separable case.

**Context comparison:** `context/notes/RESULTS_STATE.md`, Hua, findings register, 84-chi-trivialised-extension-product (conjectural, unreviewed; cited scans 146–170) should not be read as a claim that every tensor realization is faithful; this scan adds the author’s explicit failure case and qualifies that interpretation without disproving abstract existence.

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan192.tex`, scan 192, approximate TeX lines 14–36, 60–62. **Kind:** own idea.

**Brief quotation:** `b_1=\beta_1e_1\qquad c_1=\gamma_1e_1^{\unsure{\otimes2|2}}`

To globalize the scalar formula, track generator changes \(u_i'=a_i u_i+r_i\): the product generator changes by \(u'=a_1a_2u+a_1b_1r_2+a_2b_2r_1-2r_1r_2\), with the corresponding transformed monic coefficients. Confidence: high for this transition identity, verified symbolically over the integer polynomial ring; checking overlap cocycles gives a concrete route to gluing, but a full categorical associator is a further requirement.

**Context comparison:** `context/notes/RESULTS_STATE.md`, Hua, findings register, 84-chi-trivialised-extension-product (conjectural, unreviewed; cited scans 146–170) claims a global symmetric monoidal product; this is an additional local transition calculation bearing on descent, not a new proof of its coherence or invertibility assertions.

## 6. B12, literature comparisons, and additional local investigations

## SA02-310 — MPL_84_146-193#198

**Heading:** R01 — No explicit use of B12 in this packet

### R01 — No explicit use of B12 in this packet

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan170.tex`, upper definition; `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan172.tex`, opening algebra; `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan192.tex`, opening equations; comparison over scans 146–193. **Kind:** reading question / scope finding.

The positive mathematical evidence is “extensions” on scan 170 and the two equations `u_i^2+b_i u_i+c_i=0` on scan 192: this interval develops rank-two extension and algebra constructions, and contains no explicit identification of forms of P¹ with conics, rank-four Azumaya algebras, or ternary quadratic forms. The query for “SO”, “GP”, “conique”, “Azumaya”, “P¹”, “rang 3”, and related forms finds the proposed bridge in `README.md`, under B12, but not as a construction in these pages. This answers the planning question with a negative finding for the supplied interval, not a claim about the rest of cote 84 or the neighbouring cotes 77 and 82. The context labels B12 an unchecked question inferred from folder titles, so these pages change its placement rather than refute the mathematical correspondences themselves.

## SA02-311 — MPL_84_146-193#199

**Heading:** R02 — A rank-four tensor product here is not a quaternion algebra

**Index kind:** unpursued implication.

**Scans cited:** 172,192

### R02 — A rank-four tensor product here is not a quaternion algebra

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan172.tex`, upper and middle diagrams, “A₁⊗A₂”; scan 192, defining equations. **Kind:** unpursued implication.

Under the displayed quadratic-algebra interpretation, `A₁⊗A₂` has rank four but is commutative; its centre is the entire rank-four algebra, so it is not the degree-two central Azumaya algebra required by B12 over a nonzero base. The distinction matters because rank alone does not connect this tensor product with a quaternion algebra or a conic. The context search finds “Azumaya” only in `README.md`'s proposed B12, while `notes/RESULTS_STATE.md`, Hua's `84-chi-trivialised-extension-product`, instead concerns the rank-two product; the present observation separates those two leads.

## SA02-312 — MPL_84_146-193#200

**Heading:** R03 — What a future B12 identification would need

**Index kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** medium as a search direction, low as an identification of this manuscript's intent.

**Scans cited:** 160,192

### R03 — What a future B12 identification would need

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan160.tex`, “loc. libres de rang fini” and duality assertion; scan 192, algebra/module displays. **Kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** medium as a search direction, low as an identification of this manuscript's intent.

A further manuscript could connect these rank-two objects to B12 by supplying a regular ternary quadratic form and its even Clifford algebra, or a PGL₂-torsor and its associated conic, but no such connecting datum is written here. Over a field of characteristic different from two, the conic/quaternion/similarity correspondence is treated in Voight (2021, §§5.2–5.5); its hypotheses must be checked afresh before using a scheme or characteristic-two version. The context comparison is `README.md`, B12, which supplies the proposed destination but no map from the extensions on scans 160–192. The confidence is limited because the common folder title is evidence for looking, not evidence for an existing construction.

## SA02-313 — MPL_84_146-193#201

### R04 — Rank-two module data do not already provide a twisted P¹

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan160.tex`, two extension sequences and duality statement; scan 170, “E₁∨E₂” and “E₁∧E₂”. **Kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** high for the distinction; low for a connection to the author's intended later work.

If an actual rank-two vector bundle E is given, its projectivization and endomorphism algebra provide a split-type projective bundle/Azumaya example, but that construction alone supplies no nontrivial Brauer class. A proposed B12 reading therefore needs descent data or a genuinely twisted object, not merely the symbols E and A in the displayed dualities. Searching the context for “torsor”, “Azumaya”, “P¹” and “extension” gives B12 as an unchecked target and Hua's extension-product claim as the only direct match to these scans; neither supplies the missing identification. The confidence in this caution comes from the distinction between an endomorphism algebra and an arbitrary degree-two Azumaya algebra, not from an interpretation of unclear ink.

## SA02-314 — MPL_84_146-193#202

**Heading:** R05 — The local algebra product has a precise published counterpart

**Index kind:** unpursued implication / literature identification.

**Scans cited:** 172,192

### R05 — The local algebra product has a precise published counterpart

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan172.tex`, “b=b₁b₂”, “c=b₁²c₂+b₂²c₁−4c₁c₂”; scan 192, “u₁*u₂”. **Kind:** unpursued implication / literature identification.

Voight (2016, Construction 3.14, Remark 3.17) gives the same quadratic-algebra law: with his parameters t=−b₁, s=−b₂, n=c₁, m=c₂, his generator w is the manuscript's u+b₁b₂, so his relation w²−b₁b₂w+c=0 becomes u²+b₁b₂u+c=0. This is a precise local comparison, independently checked by the included polynomial calculation, and does not identify the manuscript's entire category of χ-trivialised extensions with that paper's objects. Context: Hua, findings register, `84-chi-trivialised-extension-product`, in `notes/RESULTS_STATE.md`, records a candidate without a located reference; the literature supplies a close known algebraic specialisation, while leaving the stronger all-χ statement to be checked. No priority or novelty conclusion follows from this match.

## SA02-316 — MPL_84_146-193#204

Loos (1996) constructs an associative and commutative tensor product of unital quadratic forms extending the separable quadratic-algebra product, according to the article's primary abstract. Comparing its morphisms, base point and coherence maps with the manuscript's χ-trivialisation would be a concrete next step, but the full article was not inspected here and no equivalence is asserted. ## SA02-317 — MPL_84_146-193#205

**Heading:** R08 — Binary forms with modules offer a second, different comparison

**Index kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** medium as a comparison problem, low for an equivalence without further structure.

**Scans cited:** 160,192

### R08 — Binary forms with modules offer a second, different comparison

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan192.tex`, “(x₁*x₂,e₁⊗e₂)” and the accompanying quadratic equation; scan 160, algebra/module pairings. **Kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** medium as a comparison problem, low for an equivalence without further structure.

Wood (2011, Theorems 1.4–1.6 and 2.1) relates line-bundle-valued binary quadratic forms to quadratic algebras with traceable modules over an arbitrary base, with primitive forms corresponding to invertible modules. To compare the manuscript's E with such a module, one must exhibit the algebra action, verify equality of the two trace maps, and specify the line-bundle orientation; a perfect base-module pairing alone does not provide these data. Context: this gives a possible way to formulate the invertibility clause in Hua's `84-chi-trivialised-extension-product`, but the claimed T₀ criterion is not written in the supplied interval and is not identified with Wood's primitivity criterion. The confidence is limited by those missing structures rather than by any claim of novelty.

## SA02-318 — MPL_84_146-193#206

**Heading:** R09 — No demonstrated route to the LM2 congruence questions

**Index kind:** reading question / context comparison.

**Scans cited:** 146,172

### R09 — No demonstrated route to the LM2 congruence questions

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan172.tex`, “δ=δ₁δ₂”; comparison across scans 146–193. **Kind:** reading question / context comparison.

The multiplicative discriminant formula is a calculation in quadratic algebras, whereas `LM2_CONGRUENCE_ROWS.md` concerns arithmetic and GT actions on congruence quotients, including the still-open two-coordinate realization problem `LM2 C.d5`. Neither a modular curve X(n), a regular polyhedron over a ring, nor an action of GT is constructed in these pages, so the formula supplies no demonstrated step toward C.d5, `LM2 B.f7`, or `LM2 E.3`. The common vocabulary of determinants, norms, extensions or quadratic data does not establish a map between the problems; B5 remains an unchecked planning bridge here. The other open IDs in `notes/RESULTS_STATE.md` are consequently not reported as settled or advanced by this packet.

## SA02-320 — MPL_84_146-193#208

**Heading:** R11 — A local rank-one groupoid has a multiplication without division

**Index kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** high for this declared local model, because the laws are verified as polynomial identities; identification with a global manuscript category remains conditional.

**Scans cited:** 146,154,164,170

### R11 — A local rank-one groupoid has a multiplication without division

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan146.tex`, coordinate changes; scan 164, complementary matrices; scan 170, χ-trivialisation identities. **Kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** high for this declared local model, because the laws are verified as polynomial identities; identification with a global manuscript category remains conditional.

With fixed trivial rank-one ends, write π=(χ,τ), ϖ=(−τ,χ)ᵗ and represent a change of splitting by an arrow a:τ→τ+χa; the product of objects τ,σ is τσ, and the product of arrows a,b has coordinate aσ+τb+χab. The target, composition, associativity, symmetry and unit laws are polynomial identities over the integers, verified in `records/recompute_chi_groupoid.py` and explained in `records/chi_groupoid_derivation.md`, so this local construction is a strict symmetric monoidal groupoid with unit object 1. Context: this gives a positive local mechanism for the all-χ and coefficient-product parts of Hua's `84-chi-trivialised-extension-product`, independently of the obstruction to a particular comparison map on scans 154–170. Descent for varying line bundles and identification of the manuscript's global functor still require proof.

## SA02-321 — MPL_84_146-193#209

**Heading:** R12 — The same model gives the expected local invertibility criterion

**Index kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** high for the local criterion, because both implications have an explicit algebraic witness; medium for its proposed identification with the context's global criterion.

**Scans cited:** 146,170

### R12 — The same model gives the expected local invertibility criterion

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan146.tex`, “b=b₁b₂” and coordinate changes; scan 170, extension objects; interpreted through R11. **Kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** high for the local criterion, because both implications have an explicit algebraic witness; medium for its proposed identification with the context's global criterion.

In the groupoid of R11, τ has an inverse up to isomorphism exactly when τσ+χa=1 for some σ,a∈R, equivalently (τ,χ)=R, or τ mod χ is a unit in R/(χ). Necessity reads off the arrow from the product to the unit, and sufficiency uses σ as the inverse object with that same arrow. Context: this is the local trivial-line version suggested by the “T₀ generates L on V(χ)” clause of Hua's `84-chi-trivialised-extension-product`, not evidence that the named T₀ or its global descent is supplied on these pages. The computation supports the criterion under explicit identifications, and does not establish that the separately drawn pushout and pullback products agree.

## SA02-322 — MPL_84_146-193#210

**Heading:** R13 — Automorphisms survive when χ has an annihilator

**Index kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** high within the explicit model, because automorphisms are exactly arrows whose target equals their source.

**Scans cited:** 146,154

### R13 — Automorphisms survive when χ has an annihilator

**Source:** `pages/MPL_84_146-193_84_scan146.tex`, coordinate changes; scan 154, “une indétermination”; interpreted through R11. **Kind:** own idea. **Confidence:** high within the explicit model, because automorphisms are exactly arrows whose target equals their source.

An object τ in R11 has automorphism group Ann_R(χ), so replacing this groupoid by the orbit set R/χR loses data whenever χ has nonzero annihilator. For an invertible object, multiplication by τ is invertible on that automorphism group: if τσ+χa=1, multiplication by σ is its inverse there. Context: this refines the categorical content of Hua's `84-chi-trivialised-extension-product`, whose conjectural summary does not specify how automorphisms are retained; it is also compatible with, but does not identify, scan 154's Hom-valued ambiguity. The finite-ring examples in the script illustrate this distinction without serving as a proof of the global category claim.

## 7. Standing question: which steps the author marks, checks, changes, or leaves unfinished

Editorial uncertainty and authorial uncertainty are distinguished throughout these registers. Emphasis, an asserted qualification, a completed “OK,” and a literal question mark have different evidentiary force.

### 8.1. Mathematical leaves

No title sheet, cover, independent work title, or authorial total-page count occurs on mathematical scans 146, 148, 150, 152, 154, 156, 158, 160, 162, 164, 166, 168, and 170. Their bottom pencil numbers are 145, 147, …, 169 and are transcribed as marginal numerals; they do not establish a separate authorial pagination scheme or a total of pages. Internal counts such as “2” compatibilities on scan 154 and “4” squares on scan 166 are structural counts, registered above, not page counts.

The even mathematical leaves 172–192 have no identified title sheet or explicit total page count in the frozen transcription. “compatibilités pour” (178), “Considérons” (188), and the generator question (190) are working headings or prompts; they are not titles of a separately counted work. Pencil margin numbers 171,173,…,191 identify positions but do not state the extent of a text. The reused typed pages, handled separately, contain the package’s explicit textual titles and page-count remarks.

### 8.4. Chronology and the limits of dating reused sheets

The printed month/day annotations do not print a year. **About 1984 is an internal inference**, principally from the 9 October recollection of 17 October 1976 as almost exactly eight years earlier (scan 151, lines 12–15), corroborated by the five-year retrospective and the explicit 1979 account. This dates the reused typed sequence by its own statements; it does not by itself date the mathematics on other leaves.

| Date or interval | Event or textual layer | Evidentiary status and source |
|---|---|---|
| 1933, father aged 43 | Alleged reversal in the parents' roles | Author's retrospective family interpretation; scan 153, lines 40–43 |
| 1939 | Mother said to have taken him back under pressure of events | Author's retrospective account; scan 153, lines 43–45 |
| 1942 | Father's “disparition” said to end the dependence | Retain this wording without adding external biography; scan 153, lines 45–47 |
| July 1976 | Relationship with G. and new self-acceptance before meditation | Explicit month/year; scan 171, line 10 |
| 15 October 1976, inferred | Discovery of meditation | Inferred from “surlendemain” relative to 17 October; scan 151, lines 14–19 |
| Sunday 17 October 1976 | First childhood meditation and intuition about a destructive force | Explicit date and weekday; scan 151, lines 14–24 |
| Following night/morning | Dream and its subsequent four-hour interpretation | Relative chronology only; scans 151, lines 32–38, and 155, lines 4–14 |
| Nearly three years thereafter | Alleged avoidance of the childhood inquiry | Author's interval; scan 151, lines 25–29 |
| Early 1979 | First feminine–masculine reflections before knowing the Chinese vocabulary | Explicit approximate date; scan 191, lines 17–21 |
| Late 1979, “je crois” | Learned the terms and tradition through daughter and Ahmed | Author's qualified dating; scan 191, lines 21–24 |
| Five years before the present survey | Earlier collection of approximately 100–200 pairs into ~20 groups | Relative interval; scan 175, line 4. A date around 1979 follows only if the inferred 1984 writing year is accepted. |
| Two years before the present account | Recognition of approval-seeking said to disarm it | Relative interval; scan 163, lines 31–34. Approximately 1982 is an inference, not a printed date. |
| Four days before 9 October | Previous note said to have been completed | Relative statement, scan 151, lines 12–14; 5 October is the literal calendar inference, distinct from later 6 October annotations. |
| 6 October | Footnote on persistent desire; notes 1081 and 1082 | Scans 149, lines 16–19; 153, line 4; 165, line 4 |
| 9 October | Note 109 begins | Scan 151, line 12 |
| 10 October | Continued account of gradual restoration | Scan 167, line 16 |
| 11 October | Note 110 begins | Scan 171, line 4 |
| 12 October, inferred | Main renewed survey and graph-drawing session | “Hier” in note dated 13 October; scans 161, lines 33–37, and 175, lines 4–7 |
| 13 October | Note 111 begins; late pairs arrived “ce matin” | Scans 161, line 33, and 177, lines 13–15 |
